0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.56012pt\raise-20.3125pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.43059pt\raise-25.67271pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 30.78474pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 52.1389pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{[\mathrm{Cone}(X\times S)/\mathbb{G}_{m,S}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.1389pt\raise-28.66985pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-31.61111pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 27.43059pt\raise-31.61111pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{S}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}\;\middle|\begin{array}[]{rl}(i)&\text{$q$ is a family of log Fano quasi-}\\
&\text{maps of degree $m$ and of weight $u$,}\\
(ii)&\text{$q_{\bar{s}}$ is K-semistable}\\
&\text{for any geometric point $\bar{s}\in S$,}\\
\!(iii)&\text{$r\mathcal{B}_{\bar{s}}$ is a $\mathbb{Z}$-divisor}\\
&\text{for any geometric point $\bar{s}\in S$}\\
(iv)&\text{$\mathrm{deg}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-K_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}-\mathcal{B}_{\bar{s}})-um=v$}\\
&\text{for any geometric point $\bar{s}\in S$}\\
(v)&\text{$q_{\bar{s}}$ is non-constant for any}\\
&\text{geometric point $\bar{s}\in S$}\end{array}\right\}.

Then,
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,nonconst.qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
is an open substack of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
.

Proof.

It suffices to show the following by Theorem
3.23
.
For any scheme
S
of finite type over
\mathbbm{k}
and object
q\colon(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{B})\to[\mathrm{Cone}(X)\times S/\mathbb{G}_{m,S}]
in
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}(S)
, there exists an open subset
S^{\prime}\subset S
such that for any geometric point
\bar{s}\in S
,
q_{\bar{s}}
is non-constant if and only if
\bar{s}\in S^{\prime}
.
We will show this as follows.
By Lemma
2.22
, it suffices to check the following two statements for
q\colon(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{B})\to[\mathrm{Cone}(X)\times S/\mathbb{G}_{m,S}]
over a scheme essentially of finite type over
\mathbbm{k}
.

(1)

If
S
is smooth and irreducible and
q_{\bar{\eta}}
is non-constant where
\eta
is the generic point of
S
, then there exists a non-empty open subset
U\subset S
such that
q_{\bar{s}}
is non-constant for any
\bar{s}\in U
, and

(2)

If
S
is a spectrum of discrete valuation ring
R
essentially of finite type over
\mathbbm{k}
and
q_{\bar{s}}
is non-constant, where
s\in S
is the closed point, then
q_{\bar{\eta}}
is non-constant, where
\eta
is the generic point of
S
.

Let
\mathscr{L}
be the line bundle induced by
q
.
For (1), if
q_{\bar{\eta}}
is non-constant, then we can choose two sections
f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathrm{Sec}_{q}(\mathscr{L})
such that
\mathrm{div}(f_{1,\bar{\eta}})\neq\mathrm{div}(f_{2,\bar{\eta}})
.
By shrinking
S
, we may assume that
\mathrm{div}(f_{1})
and
\mathrm{div}(f_{2})
are flat over
S
.
It is not hard to see that
\mathrm{div}(f_{1,\bar{s}})\neq\mathrm{div}(f_{2,\bar{s}})
is an open condition in
S
.
Therefore, there exists an open subset
U\subset S
such that
\mathrm{div}(f_{1,\bar{s}})\neq\mathrm{div}(f_{2,\bar{s}})
for any
\bar{s}\in U
.
This implies that
q_{\bar{s}}
is non-constant for any
\bar{s}\in U
.

For (2), if
q_{\bar{s}}
is non-constant, then we can choose two sections
f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathrm{Sec}_{q}(\mathscr{L})
such that
\mathrm{div}(f_{1,\bar{s}})\neq\mathrm{div}(f_{2,\bar{s}})
.
The condition
\mathrm{div}(f_{1,\bar{s}})\neq\mathrm{div}(f_{2,\bar{s}})
is open and hence
\mathrm{div}(f_{1,\bar{\eta}})\neq\mathrm{div}(f_{2,\bar{\eta}})
.
This implies that
q_{\bar{\eta}}
is non-constant.

Since (1) and (2) hold, we obtain the assertion.
∎

3.3.
\Theta
-reductivity and
\mathsf{S}
-completeness

In this subsection, we deal with the
\Theta
-reductivity and
\mathsf{S}
-completeness for
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
.

We prepare some notations.
For a scheme
S
, we set

\Theta_{S}:=[\mathbb{A}^{1}_{S}/\mathbb{G}_{m,S}].

Let
R
be a discrete valuation ring with the fractional field
K
, and let
\pi\in R
be a generator of the maximal ideal.
For the simplicity of notation,
\Theta_{\mathrm{Spec}R}
is denoted by
\Theta_{R}
.
We also define an open subset
\Theta_{R}^{\circ}\subset\Theta_{R}
by

\Theta_{R}^{\circ}:=\Theta_{\mathrm{Spec}\,K}\cup[\mathbb{G}_{m,R}/\mathbb{G}_{m,R}].

We set

\mathrm{ST}(R):=[\mathrm{Spec}(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))/\mathbb{G}_{m,R}],

where
\mathbb{G}_{m,R}
acts on
R[s,t]/(st-\pi)
in the way that

R[s,t]/(st-\pi)\ni s,t\mapsto s\otimes\mu,t\otimes\mu^{-1}\in R[s,t]/(st-\pi)\otimes_{R}R[\mu,\mu^{-1}].

Let
\mathbf{0}\in\mathrm{Spec}(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))
be the point corresponding to the maximal ideal generated by
\pi
,
s
, and
t
.
We put

\mathrm{ST}(R)^{\circ}:=[(\mathrm{Spec}(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\})/\mathbb{G}_{m,R}].

Definition 3.27

(
\Theta
-reductivity and
\mathsf{S}
-completeness)
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a stack over
\mathbbm{k}
such that
\mathcal{M}=[X/G]
for some scheme
X
of finite type over
\mathbbm{k}
and linear algebraic group
G
over
\mathbbm{k}
.
We say that

•

\mathcal{M}
satisfies
\Theta
-reductivity
if for any discrete valuation ring
R
and morphism
\varphi^{\circ}\colon\Theta_{R}^{\circ}\to\mathcal{M}
, there exists a unique extension
\varphi\colon\Theta_{R}\to\mathcal{M}
of
\varphi^{\circ}
, and

•

\mathcal{M}
satisfies
\mathsf{S}
-completeness
if for any discrete valuation ring
R
and morphism
\psi^{\circ}\colon\mathrm{ST}(R)^{\circ}\to\mathcal{M}
, there exists a unique extension
\psi\colon\mathrm{ST}(R)\to\mathcal{M}
of
\psi^{\circ}
.

By
[
AHLH23
, Propositions 3.18 and 3.42]
, if the above conditions hold at least for any discrete valuation ring
R
essentially of finite type over
\mathbbm{k}
, then both conditions hold.

Theorem 3.28
.

Fix a closed embedding of a projective normal variety
\iota\colon X\subset\mathbb{P}^{N}
,
v,u\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
and
r,m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.
We assume
0<u<1-\frac{v}{2}
.
Then
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
satisfies
\Theta
-reductivity and
\mathsf{S}
-completeness.

Proof.

We first deal with
\mathsf{S}
-completeness.
Let
R
be a discrete valuation ring essentially of finite type over
\mathbbm{k}
, and let
\psi^{\circ}\colon\mathrm{ST}(R)^{\circ}\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
be an arbitrary morphism.
Then the datum of
\psi^{\circ}
corresponds to
q^{\circ}\colon(Y^{\circ},B^{\circ})\to[\mathrm{Cone}(X)/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant family of K-semistable log Fano quasimaps over
\mathrm{Spec}(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
of weight
u
and degree
m
.
Thus, it suffices to show that there exists a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant family of K-semistable log Fano quasimaps
q\colon(Y,B)\to[\mathrm{Cone}(X)/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
over
\mathrm{Spec}(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))
such that
q|_{\mathrm{Spec}(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}=q^{\circ}
unique up to isomorphism.
Take a general vector
(a_{i})_{i=0}^{N}\in\mathbbm{k}^{N+1}
and its corresponding section
f^{\circ}\in H^{0}(Y^{\circ},\mathscr{L}^{\circ})
, where
\mathscr{L}^{\circ}
is the line bundle induced by
q^{\circ}
.
We note that
f^{\circ}
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant.
By the choice of
f^{\circ}
, we see that
g^{\circ}\colon(Y^{\circ},B^{\circ}+u\mathrm{div}(f^{\circ}))\to\mathrm{Spec}(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
is a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant family of K-semistable log pairs by Lemma
3.12
.
By
[
ABHLX20
, Theorem 3.3]
and its proof,
g^{\circ}
is extended to a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant family of K-semistable log Fano pairs

g\colon(Y,B+uD)\to\mathrm{Spec}(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))

unique up to isomorphism, where
B
is the closure of
B^{\circ}
and
D
is the closure of
\mathrm{div}(f^{\circ})
.
Since the codimension of
Y\setminus Y^{\circ}
is two and any fiber of
g
is a normal curve, there exists a unique extension
\mathscr{L}
of
\mathscr{L}^{\circ}
as a line bundle.
Then we obtain the canonical isomorphism
H^{0}(Y,\mathscr{L})\cong H^{0}(Y^{\circ},\mathscr{L}^{\circ})
.
Therefore, we see that there exists the unique extension
f\in H^{0}(Y,\mathscr{L})
of
f^{\circ}
and
\mathrm{div}(f)
does not contain
Y\setminus Y^{\circ}
.
Furthermore, the homomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{Y^{\circ}}^{N+1}\to\mathscr{L}^{\circ}
induced by
q^{\circ}
is uniquely extended to a homomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{Y}^{N+1}\to\mathscr{L}
.
This shows that we have an extension
q\colon(Y,B)\to[\mathrm{Cone}(X)/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
as a family of K-semistable log Fano quasimaps.

Next, we show that the extension
q
of
q^{\circ}
is unique. Take
q^{\prime}\colon(Y^{\prime},B^{\prime})\to[\mathrm{Cone}(X)/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
another extension.
Let
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}
be the line bundle associated to
q^{\prime}
. Since the codimension of
Y^{\prime}\setminus Y^{\circ}
is two, we see that
H^{0}(Y^{\prime},\mathscr{L}^{\prime})\cong H^{0}(Y^{\circ},\mathscr{L}^{\circ})
.
We choose
f^{\circ}\in H^{0}(Y^{\circ},\mathscr{L}^{\circ})
corresponding to a general vector
(a_{i})_{i=0}^{N}\in\mathbbm{k}^{N+1}
such that
(Y_{\mathbf{0}},B_{\mathbf{0}}+u\mathrm{div}(f)_{\mathbf{0}})
and
(Y^{\prime}_{\mathbf{0}},B^{\prime}_{\mathbf{0}}+u\mathrm{div}(f^{\prime})_{\mathbf{0}})
are K-semistable, where
f
and
f^{\prime}
correspond to the extensions of
f^{\circ}
.
By
[
ABHLX20
, Theorem 3.3]
, the families
g\colon(Y,B+u\mathrm{div}(f))\to\mathrm{Spec}(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))
and
g^{\prime}\colon(Y^{\prime},B^{\prime}+u\mathrm{div}(f^{\prime}))\to\mathrm{Spec}(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))
are isomorphic to each other.
By
H^{0}(Y^{\prime},\mathscr{L}^{\prime})\cong H^{0}(Y^{\circ},\mathscr{L}^{\circ})
, the quasimap structures
q
and
q^{\prime}
are also isomorphic to each other in the natural way.
These arguments imply the
\mathsf{S}
-completeness.

On the other hand, the
\Theta
-reductivity follows from a similar argument as above by reducing to the log Fano pair case
[
ABHLX20
, Theorem 5.2]
.
Thus, we complete the proof.
∎

It is easy to see that Theorem
3.28
does not hold for log–twisted K-stability by the following easy example.
Example
3.29
states that to construct “a moduli of log–twisted K-semistable Fano pairs” via the good moduli theory, we need some additional information, for example the quasimap structure.

Example 3.29
.

Consider a log Fano pair
(\mathbb{P}^{1},\frac{1}{2}p)
for some closed point
p\in\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
Note that if
(\mathbb{P}^{1},\frac{1}{2}p)
degenerates to a log Fano pair, then the degeneration is isomorphic to
(\mathbb{P}^{1},\frac{1}{2}p)
.
Furthermore, the log–twisted Fano pair
(\mathbb{P}^{1},\frac{1}{2}p,-\frac{1}{4}K_{\mathbb{P}^{1}})
is twisted K-polystable in the sense of
[
BLZ22
, Definition 3.2]
even though
(\mathbb{P}^{1},\frac{1}{2}p)
is K-unstable.
Here, the stack
[\mathrm{Spec}\mathbbm{k}/\mathbb{G}_{a}]
is isomorphic to the moduli stack of klt log Fano pairs degenerated from
(\mathbb{P}^{1},\frac{1}{2}p)
.
If the family of
(\mathbb{P}^{1},\frac{1}{2}p)
satisfies the
\mathsf{S}
-completeness, this contradicts
[
AHLH23
, Proposition 3.47]
.
This means that a family of log–twisted K-semistable Fano pairs does not satisfy the
\mathsf{S}
-completeness in general without any quasimap structure.

3.4.
Properness

In this subsection, we deal with the properness of the good moduli space of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
.
We will show the properness in a different way from
[
BHLLX21
]
, i.e., without using the
\Theta
-stratification.

Lemma 3.30
.

Let
R
be a discrete valuation ring essentially of finite type over
\mathbbm{k}
with the fractional field
K
.
Let
q\colon(Y,B)\to[\mathbb{A}^{N+1}_{K}/\mathbb{G}_{m,K}]
be a family of log Fano quasimaps over
\mathrm{Spec}\,K
of degree
m
and of weight
u
such that
Y_{\overline{K}}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\overline{K}}
, where
\overline{K}
is the algebraic closure of
K
.

Then there exists a finite field extension
K\subset K^{\prime}
and the integral closure
R^{\prime}
of
R
in
K^{\prime}
satisfying the following.

•

there exists a family of log Fano quasimaps
\bar{q}\colon(Y_{R^{\prime}},B_{R^{\prime}})\to[\mathbb{A}^{N+1}_{R^{\prime}}/\mathbb{G}_{m,R^{\prime}}]
,

•

an arbitrary closed point
r^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Spec}\,R^{\prime}
is mapped to the closed point
r\in\mathrm{Spec}\,R
, and

•

the restriction
\bar{q}|_{\mathrm{Spec}(K^{\prime})}\colon(Y_{K^{\prime}},B_{K^{\prime}})\to[\mathbb{A}^{N+1}_{K^{\prime}}/\mathbb{G}_{m,K^{\prime}}]
coincides with the base change of
q\colon Y\to[\mathbb{A}^{N+1}_{K}/\mathbb{G}_{m,K}]
.

Proof.

By the assumption and Lemma
2.60
, there exists a
finite field extension
K^{\prime\prime}
of
K
such that
Y\times_{\mathrm{Spec}K}\mathrm{Spec}K^{\prime\prime}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}_{K^{\prime\prime}}
.
Let
R^{\prime\prime}
be the integral closure of
R
in
K^{\prime\prime}
and let
\bar{r}
be a geometric point of
\mathrm{Spec}R^{\prime\prime}
supported on a closed point.
Let
\mathscr{L}_{K^{\prime\prime}}
be the associated line bundle with
q_{K^{\prime\prime}}
.
Take the corresponding section
f_{j}\in\mathrm{Sec}_{q_{K^{\prime\prime}}}(\mathscr{L}_{K^{\prime\prime}})
to the
j
-th coordinate function on the canonical basis of
\mathbb{A}_{\mathbbm{k}}^{N+1}
for
1\leq j\leq N+1
.
By an open immersion
Y_{K^{\prime\prime}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R^{\prime\prime}}
, we can regard
f_{j}
as a rational section of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R^{\prime\prime}}}(m)
.
Let
r^{\prime\prime}
be a closed point of
\mathrm{Spec}\,R^{\prime\prime}
,
\pi^{\prime\prime}_{r^{\prime\prime}}
a generator of the maximal ideal of
R^{\prime\prime}
corresponding to
r^{\prime\prime}
and
\mu_{r^{\prime\prime}}:=\min_{f_{j}}\mathrm{coeff}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{r^{\prime\prime}}}(\mathrm{div}(f_{j}))
.
Here, we may regard all
f_{j}
as regular sections of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R^{\prime\prime}}}(m)
and assume that there exists
f_{j}
that has neither any zero nor pole along
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{r^{\prime\prime}}
by replacing
f_{j}
’s with
f_{j}\cdot\prod_{r^{\prime\prime}}(\pi^{\prime\prime}_{r^{\prime\prime}})^{-\mu_{r^{\prime\prime}}}
.
Let
B^{\prime\prime}
be the Zariski closure of
B\times_{\mathrm{Spec}K}\mathrm{Spec}K^{\prime\prime}
in
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R^{\prime\prime}}
.
Now,
f_{j}
’s define a quasimap structure
q_{R^{\prime\prime}}\colon(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R^{\prime\prime}},B^{\prime\prime})\to[\mathbb{A}^{N+1}_{R^{\prime\prime}}/\mathbb{G}_{m,R^{\prime\prime}}]
but note that
q_{R^{\prime\prime}}
is not a family of log Fano quasimaps in general.
Now we prove the following claim.

Claim
.

There exists a finite extended field
K^{\prime}
of
K^{\prime\prime}
satisfying the following for any general
f=\sum a_{i}f_{i}
, where
a_{i}\in\mathbbm{k}
.

(1)

Let
R^{\prime}
be the integral closure of
R^{\prime\prime}
in
K^{\prime}
. Then, there exists a family
\pi^{\prime}\colon(Y^{\prime},B^{\prime}+D^{\prime})\to\mathrm{Spec}R^{\prime}
such that
Y^{\prime}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R^{\prime}}
,
(Y^{\prime},B^{\prime}+D^{\prime}+Y^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}})
is plt and
-(K_{Y^{\prime}}+B^{\prime}+D^{\prime})
is
\pi^{\prime}
-ample, where
r^{\prime}
is an arbitrary closed point of
\mathrm{Spec}\,R^{\prime}
, and

(2)

there exists a birational map
\xi\colon(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R^{\prime\prime}},B^{\prime\prime}+u\mathrm{div}(f))\times_{\mathrm{Spec}\,R^{\prime\prime}}\mathrm{Spec}\,R^{\prime}\dashrightarrow(Y^{\prime},B^{\prime}+D^{\prime})
, where
D^{\prime}=u\xi_{*}(\mathrm{div}(f))
and
B^{\prime}=\xi_{*}(B^{\prime\prime}\times_{\mathrm{Spec}\,R^{\prime\prime}}\mathrm{Spec}\,R^{\prime})
.

Furthermore, the construction of
(Y^{\prime},B^{\prime})
does not depend on the choice of general
f
.

Proof.

By the semistable reduction theorem (cf.
[
KKMSD73
]
), there exists a finite surjective morphism
\mathrm{Spec}\,R^{\prime}\to\mathrm{Spec}\,R^{\prime\prime}
for some
R^{\prime}
such that the base change
(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R^{\prime\prime}},B^{\prime\prime}+u\mathrm{div}(f))\times_{\mathrm{Spec}\,R^{\prime\prime}}\mathrm{Spec}\,R^{\prime}
admits a semistable resolution

\varphi\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R^{\prime}}\times_{\mathrm{Spec}\,R^{\prime\prime}}\mathrm{Spec}\,R^{\prime}.

We note that this semistable reduction can be taken as the same one for any general
f
.
By defining
K^{\prime}
to be the fractional field of
K^{\prime\prime}
, we obtain a finite field extension
K^{\prime}\supset K^{\prime\prime}
.
By definition,
\varphi
is projective and birational and

(\mathcal{X},\varphi^{-1}_{*}(B^{\prime\prime}\times_{\mathrm{Spec}\,R^{\prime\prime}}\mathrm{Spec}\,R^{\prime}+u\mathrm{div}(f))+\mathcal{X}_{r^{\prime}})

is log smooth and dlt, where
r^{\prime}
is an arbitrary closed point of
\mathrm{Spec}\,R^{\prime}
.
Replacing
R^{\prime}
by the integral closure of
R^{\prime\prime}
in
K^{\prime}
, we may assume that
R^{\prime}
is the integral closure of
R^{\prime\prime}
in
K^{\prime}
.
Next,
we run the minimal model program of
(\mathcal{X},\varphi^{-1}_{*}(B^{\prime\prime\prime}+u\mathrm{div}(f))+\mathcal{X}_{r^{\prime}})
over
\mathrm{Spec}\,R^{\prime}
and it ends with a Mori fiber space

\psi\colon(\mathcal{X},\varphi^{-1}_{*}(B^{\prime\prime\prime}+u\mathrm{div}(f))+\mathcal{X}_{r^{\prime}})\dashrightarrow(Y^{\prime},\psi_{*}(\varphi^{-1}_{*}(B^{\prime\prime\prime}+u\mathrm{div}(f)))+Y^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}})

for any closed point
r^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Spec}\,R^{\prime}
, where
B^{\prime\prime\prime}
denotes
B^{\prime\prime}\times_{\mathrm{Spec}\,R^{\prime\prime}}\mathrm{Spec}\,R^{\prime}
by
[
BCHM10
]
and Remark
2.6
.
Since
Y^{\prime}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial and the relative Picard number
\rho(Y^{\prime}/\mathrm{Spec}\,R^{\prime})=1
,
Y^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}
is irreducible.
Hence,
(Y^{\prime},\psi_{*}(\varphi^{-1}_{*}(B^{\prime\prime\prime}+u\mathrm{div}(f)))+Y^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}})
is plt and
-K_{Y^{\prime}}-\psi_{*}(\varphi^{-1}_{*}(B^{\prime\prime\prime}+u\mathrm{div}(f)))
is relatively ample for any
r^{\prime}
.
Since the minimal model program as above depends only on the relative
\mathbb{R}
-linear equivalence class of
\varphi^{-1}_{*}(B^{\prime\prime\prime}+u\mathrm{div}(f))+\mathcal{X}_{r^{\prime}}
,
\psi
does not depend on the choice of
r^{\prime}
or
f
.
Moreover,
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\overline{r^{\prime}}}\cong Y^{\prime}_{\overline{r^{\prime}}}
since
Y^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}
is one dimensional and normal, and
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{K^{\prime}}\cong Y^{\prime}_{K^{\prime}}
by assumption.
Note that the canonical morphism
\pi_{Y^{\prime}}\colon Y^{\prime}\to\mathrm{Spec}\,R^{\prime}
is smooth and projective.
Therefore, we can take a line bundle
\mathcal{L}
on
Y^{\prime}
such that
\mathcal{L}|_{Y^{\prime}_{K^{\prime}}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{K^{\prime}}}(1)
.
It is easy to see that
h^{0}(Y^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}},\mathcal{L}_{r^{\prime}})=2
and
h^{1}(Y^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}},\mathcal{L}_{r^{\prime}})=0
for any closed point
r^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Spec}\,R^{\prime}
and hence
Y^{\prime}\cong\mathbb{P}_{R^{\prime}}(\pi_{Y^{\prime},*}\mathcal{L})
.
Thus, we obtain the assertion.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.30
continued.

Let
f=\sum a_{i}f_{i}
be a general section and take
Y^{\prime}
as in the claim.
We may assume that
\mathrm{coeff}_{Y^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}}(\mathrm{div}(f))=\min_{h\in\mathrm{Sec}_{q_{K^{\prime}}\setminus\{0\}}(\mathscr{L}_{K^{\prime}})}\mathrm{coeff}_{Y^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}}(\mathrm{div}(h))
by perturbing
f
.
By taking a section
g\in H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R^{\prime}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R^{\prime}}}(m))
such that
u\mathrm{div}(g)=D^{\prime}
and
g_{j}\in H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R^{\prime}},\mathcal{O}(m))
such that
g_{j}=g\cdot\frac{f_{j}}{f}
.
We see that
g_{j}
is a well-defined section.
Then, it is easy to see that
g_{j}
’s define a quasimap structure
q^{\prime}\colon Y^{\prime}\to[\mathbb{A}^{N+1}_{R^{\prime}}/\mathbb{G}_{m,R^{\prime}}]
as desired.
∎

Theorem 3.31
.

Let
R
be a discrete valuation ring essentially of finite type over
\mathbbm{k}
with the fractional field
K
.
Let
q\colon Y\to[\mathbb{A}^{N+1}_{K}/\mathbb{G}_{m,K}]
be a quasimap over
\mathrm{Spec}\,K
such that
Y_{\overline{K}}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\overline{K}}
of degree
m
and of weight
u
, where
\overline{K}
is the algebraic closure of
K
.
Let
B
be an effective
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor on
Y
and suppose that
q_{\overline{K}}\colon(Y_{\overline{K}},B_{\overline{K}})\to[\mathbb{A}^{N+1}_{\overline{K}}/\mathbb{G}_{m,\overline{K}}]
is a K-semistable log Fano quasimap.
Suppose that
0<u<\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathrm{deg}_{\mathbb{P}_{K}^{1}}(B_{K})+um\right)
.

Then there exists a finite field extension
K^{\prime}
of
K
and the integral closure
R^{\prime}
of
R
in
K^{\prime}
such that there exists a family of quasimap
\bar{q}\colon Y_{R^{\prime}}\to[\mathbb{A}^{N+1}_{R^{\prime}}/\mathbb{G}_{m,R^{\prime}}]
and an effective
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor
B_{R^{\prime}}
on
Y_{R^{\prime}}
satisfying the following:

•

the restriction
\bar{q}|_{\mathrm{Spec}(K^{\prime})}\colon(Y_{K^{\prime}},B_{K^{\prime}})\to[\mathbb{A}^{N+1}_{K^{\prime}}/\mathbb{G}_{m,K^{\prime}}]
coincides with the base change of
q\colon Y\to[\mathbb{A}^{N+1}_{K}/\mathbb{G}_{m,K}]
,

•

\bar{q}\colon(Y_{R^{\prime}},B_{R^{\prime}})\to[\mathbb{A}^{N+1}_{R^{\prime}}/\mathbb{G}_{m,R^{\prime}}]
is a family of K-semistable log Fano quasimaps.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.30
and replacing
R
, we may assume that
Y_{R}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R}
and there exists a family of klt log Fano quasimaps

q_{R}\colon(Y_{R},B_{R})\to[\mathbb{A}^{N+1}_{R}/\mathbb{G}_{m,R}].

Note that
R
may not be a discrete valuation ring anymore.
Let
\bar{r}
be a geometric point in
\mathrm{Spec}\,R
supported on a closed point.
Suppose that the fiber
q_{\bar{r}}\colon(Y_{\bar{r}},B_{\bar{r}})\to[\mathbb{A}^{N+1}_{\bar{r}}/\mathbb{G}_{m,{\bar{r}}}]
of
q_{R}
over
\bar{r}
is K-unstable.
Let
\mathscr{L}
be the line bundle on
Y_{R}=\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R}
associated with
q_{R}
.
Set
\mu:=\mathrm{deg}(B_{{\bar{r}}})+um
and take a general section
f\in\mathrm{Sec}_{q_{R}}(\mathscr{L})
, and put
D_{f}:=\mathrm{div}_{\mathscr{L}}(f)
.
Since
q_{\bar{r}}
is K-unstable, Lemma
3.12
implies that
(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\bar{r}},B_{{\bar{r}}}+u(D_{f})_{\bar{r}})
is K-unstable and there exists the unique closed point
s\in\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\bar{r}}
such that
\mathrm{mult}_{s}(B_{{\bar{r}}}+u(D_{f})_{\bar{r}})>\frac{\mu}{2}
.
Note that
\mathrm{mult}_{s}(B_{{\bar{r}}}+u(D_{f})_{\bar{r}})
is independent from the choice of general
f
.
Without loss of generality, we may assume that any irreducible component
S
of
\mathrm{Supp}(B_{R}+uD_{f})
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Spec}\,R
. Indeed, if there exists an irreducible component
S
non-isomorphic to
\mathrm{Spec}\,R
, then we may replace
\mathrm{Spec}\,R
with the normalization of
S
.

From now, we show that we can replace the family
q_{R}
of log Fano quasimaps by a family of K-semistable log Fano quasimaps.
We construct a sequence of blow-ups
h_{i}\colon Y_{i}\to Y_{i-1}
at the smooth closed point of
Y_{i-1}
, where
Y_{0}:=Y_{R}
.
Put
B_{0}^{(f)}:=B_{R}+uD_{f}
.
First, take the blow-up
h_{1}\colon Y_{1}\to Y_{R}
at the point
s
.
Let
E_{1}
be the exceptional divisor, and set
B_{1}^{(f)}:=(h_{1})_{*}^{-1}B_{0}^{(f)}
.
Suppose that we have constructed
h_{k}\colon Y_{k}\to Y_{k-1}
with the exceptional divisor
E_{k}
.
Set
B_{k}^{(f)}:=(h_{k})_{*}^{-1}B^{(f)}_{k-1}
.
If
E_{k}
and
B_{k}^{(f)}
intersect at a closed point
s_{k}
with multiplicity bigger than
\frac{\mu}{2}
for any general
f
, then let
h_{k+1}\colon Y_{k+1}\to Y_{k}
be the blow-up at
s_{k}
.
Here, we note that the closed point
s_{k}
and the intersection multiplicity of
E_{k}
and
B_{k}^{(f)}
at
s_{k}
is independent from the choice of general
f
.
It is not hard to see that if
s_{k}
exists, then
E_{k}
and
B_{k}^{(f)}
intersect with multiplicity less than
\frac{\mu}{2}
at other points since any irreducible component of
B_{k}^{(f)}
is a section over
\mathrm{Spec}\,R
.
We note that all irreducible components of
B_{k}^{(f)}
have multiplicity at most
\frac{\mu}{2}
since
q
is a family of K-semistable log Fano quasimaps.
By the theorem of resolution of singularities of a curve embedded in a regular surface, this procedure stops at some
k
.
At this
k
, we contract all irreducible components
Y_{k,\bar{r}}
but
E_{k}
and construct the family of log Fano pairs
(Y^{\prime}_{R},B^{\prime}_{R}+u(D^{(f)})^{\prime})
, where
B^{\prime}_{R}
and
(D^{(f)})^{\prime}
are the strict transforms of
B_{R}
and
D^{(f)}
for any general
f
respectively.
We check that
(Y^{\prime}_{\bar{r}},B^{\prime}_{\bar{r}}+u(D^{(f)})^{\prime}_{\bar{r}})
is K-semistable for any general
f
.
For this, it is enough to show that
B^{\prime}_{\bar{r}}+u(D^{(f)})^{\prime}_{\bar{r}}
has multiplicity at most
\frac{\mu}{2}
for any point.
Let
h\colon Y_{k}\to Y^{\prime}_{R}
be the contraction.
By the definition of
h_{k}
, we see that
B^{\prime}_{\bar{r}}+u(D^{(f)})^{\prime}_{\bar{r}}
has the multiplicity at most
\frac{\mu}{2}
for any point over which
h
is isomorphic.
On the other hand, we see that
h_{*}^{-1}(B^{\prime}+u(D^{(f)})^{\prime})\cdot(Y_{k,\bar{r}}-E_{k})\leq\frac{\mu}{2}
by the definition of
h_{k-1}
.
This shows that
(Y^{\prime}_{\bar{r}},B^{\prime}_{\bar{r}}+u(D^{(f)})^{\prime}_{\bar{r}})
is K-semistable.

We know that we can take a general
f\in\mathrm{Sec}_{q_{R}}(\mathscr{L})
such that for any
g\in\mathrm{Sec}_{q_{R}}(\mathscr{L})
,
\frac{g}{f}
has no pole at
E_{k}
as a rational function.
Fix such
f
.
We take
f^{\prime}\in H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{Y^{\prime}_{R}}((D^{(f)})^{\prime}))
such that
\mathrm{div}(f^{\prime})=(D^{(f)})^{\prime}
.
Let
f_{i}\in\mathrm{Sec}_{q_{R}}(\mathscr{L}_{R})
be the section corresponding to the canonical coordinate sections of
\mathbb{A}^{N+1}
.
Then, we see that
g_{i}:=\frac{f_{i}}{f}f^{\prime}\in H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{Y^{\prime}_{R}}((D^{(f)})^{\prime}))
and
g_{i}
’s define a quasimap structure
q^{\prime}_{R}\colon Y^{\prime}_{R}\to[\mathbb{A}_{R}^{N+1}/\mathbb{G}_{m,R}]
.
By the choice of
q^{\prime}_{R}
,
q^{\prime}_{\bar{r}}
is K-semistable and
q^{\prime}_{R}|_{\mathrm{Spec}\,K}=q
.

By repeating this procedure for all closed points of
\mathrm{Spec}\,R
, we complete the proof.
∎

Corollary 3.32
.

Fix a closed embedding of a projective normal variety
\iota\colon X\subset\mathbb{P}^{N}
,
v,u\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
and
r,m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.
We also assume that
0<u<1-\frac{v}{2}
.
Then,
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
admits a good moduli space
M^{\mathrm{Kps,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
proper over
\mathbbm{k}
.
Furthermore, there is a natural bijection of the set of
\mathbbm{k}
-valued points of
M^{\mathrm{Kps,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
and the set of the K-polystable quasimaps.

Proof.

To show the assertions, we may replace
M^{\mathrm{Kps,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
with
M^{\mathrm{Kps,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}}
and assume that
X=\mathbb{P}^{N}
by Remark
3.24
and
[
Alp13
, Lemma 4.14]
.
By Theorem
3.23
, the first assertion in this case immediately follows from
[
AHLH23
, Theorem A]
, Theorems
3.28
and
3.31
.
The last assertion follows from Lemma
3.20
.
∎

3.5.
Positivity of the CM line bundle

Here, we discuss on the definition of CM line bundles of families of log Fano quasimaps and their positivity.
We fix a normal closed subvariety
\iota\colon X\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{N}
.

Definition 3.33

(Maximal valuation on a family of quasimaps)
.

Let
S
be a normal variety and
q\colon(Y,B)\to[\mathrm{Cone}(X)/\mathbb{G}_{m,S}]
a family of K-semistable log Fano quasimaps of degree
m
and of weight
u
over
S
.
We say that the family
q
has
maximal variation
if for any very general point
p_{0}\in S
, irreducible curve
C\subset S
passing through
p_{0}
and two general distinct points
p_{1}
and
p_{2}\in C
,
q_{p_{1}}
and
q_{p_{2}}
are not isomorphic.

Definition 3.34

(CM line bundle)
.

Let
q\colon(Y,B)\to[\mathrm{Cone}(X)\times S/\mathbb{G}_{m,S}]
be a family of log Fano quasimaps of degree
m
and of weight
u
over a scheme
S
of finite type over
\mathbbm{k}
.
Let
\pi\colon Y\to S
be the canonical morphism and
\mathscr{L}
the line bundle on
Y
induced by
q
.
Take a sufficiently divisible
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
such that
-\ell(K_{Y/S}+B)-\ell u\mathscr{L}
is a
\pi
-ample Cartier divisor.
By the Knudsen–Mumford expansion
[
KM98
]
, there exist line bundles
\lambda_{0}
,
\lambda_{1}
, and
\lambda_{2}
such that for any
m>0
, we have

\mathrm{det}\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}(-m(\ell(K_{Y/S}+B)+\ell u\mathscr{L}))\sim\lambda_{0}^{\otimes\binom{m}{2}}\otimes\lambda_{1}^{\otimes m}\otimes\lambda_{2}.

We note that
\lambda_{0}
,
\lambda_{1}
, and
\lambda_{2}
are uniquely determined up to linear equivalence.
Moreover, for any morphism
g\colon T\to S
from a scheme, we have

\mathrm{det}\pi_{T,*}\mathcal{O}_{Y_{T}}(-m(\ell(K_{Y_{T}/T}+B_{T})+\ell u\mathscr{L}_{T}))\sim g^{*}\lambda_{0}^{\otimes\binom{m}{2}}\otimes g^{*}\lambda_{1}^{\otimes m}\otimes g^{*}\lambda_{2}.

Then, we define the
CM line bundle
of the log Fano quasimap
q
by

\lambda_{\mathrm{CM},\pi,q}:=\frac{-2}{\ell^{2}}\lambda_{0}.

This is a
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle over
S
and independent from the choice of
\ell
.
We note that for any morphism
h\colon C\to S
from a proper smooth curve, we have

\mathrm{deg}_{C}(h^{*}\lambda_{\mathrm{CM},\pi,q})=-(K_{Y_{C}/C}+B_{C}+u\mathscr{L}_{C})^{2}.

We show the following property of non-constant quasimaps (cf. Definition
3.4
).

Lemma 3.35
.

Let
q\colon(Y,B)\to[\mathrm{Cone}(X)\times C/\mathbb{G}_{m,C}]
be a family of log Fano quasimaps of weight
u
and degree
m
with the maximal variation over a projective curve
C
.
Let
a_{0},\ldots,a_{N+1}\in\mathbbm{k}^{N+1}
be vectors such that
\{a_{0},\ldots,a_{N}\}
is a basis, and let
\mathscr{L}
be the line bundle associated to
q
.
Let
f_{j}\in\mathrm{Sec}_{q}(\mathscr{L})
be the section corresponding to
a_{j}
for each
j=0,\ldots,N+1
.
Suppose that for a point
c\in C
, we have
f_{j,c}\neq 0
for any
j=0,\ldots,N+1
and
\mathrm{div}(f_{j,c})\neq\mathrm{div}(f_{N+1,c})
for any
j=0,\ldots,N
.

Then, the family of pairs

\sigma\colon\left(Y,\mathrm{Supp}(B)+\sum_{j=1}^{N+1}\mathrm{div}(f_{j})_{\mathrm{hor}}+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathrm{div}(f_{j}+f_{N+1})_{\mathrm{hor}}\right)\to C

has maximal variation (cf.
[
Hat25b
, Definition 2.20]
).

Proof.

Assume that
\sigma
does not have the maximal variation.
Then all general fibers of
\sigma
are isomorphic to each other.
Put
D:=\mathrm{Supp}(B)+\sum_{j=1}^{N+1}\mathrm{div}(f_{j})+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathrm{div}(f_{j}+f_{N+1})
.
We may assume that
Y=\mathbb{P}^{1}\times C
and
D
is horizontal by shrinking
C
if necessary.
Consider the following scheme

I:=\mathrm{Isom}_{C\times C}((Y,D)\times C,C\times(Y,D)),

which is of finite type over
C\times C
since for any point
p\in C\times C
, there exists an open neighborhood
p\in U\subset C\times C
,
I|_{U}
is a closed subscheme of
PGL(2)\times U
.

By the assumption, the canonical morphism
\mu\colon I\to C\times C
has an dense and constructible image
\mu(I)
.
Thus, there exists a non-empty open subset
U\subset C\times C
such that
U\subset\mu(I)
and
I|_{U}
is a closed subscheme of
PGL(2)\times U
.
Let
p_{1}\colon C\times C\to C
and
p_{2}\colon C\times C\to C
be the first and the second projections, respectively.
The condition for
c\in C
that
f_{j,c}\neq 0
for any
j=0,\ldots,N+1
and
\mathrm{div}(f_{j,c})\neq\mathrm{div}(f_{N+1,c})
for any
j=0,\ldots,N
is open.
Thus, we can take a closed point
c\in p_{1}(U)
such that
f_{j,c}\neq 0
for any
j=0,\ldots,N+1
and
\mathrm{div}(f_{j,c})\neq\mathrm{div}(f_{N+1,c})
for any
j=0,\ldots,N
.
By regarding
I
as a
C
-scheme under
p_{1}
, we can consider the fiber
I_{c}
of
I
over
c\in p_{1}(U)
.
Let
\nu\colon I_{c}\to C
be the composition of the canonical morphism
I_{c}\to C\times C
and
p_{2}
.
Since
U\cap p_{1}^{-1}(c)\subset p_{2}^{-1}(\nu(I_{c}))
, there exists a morphism
\psi\colon V\to C
from an affine smooth curve such that
\xi\colon V\times_{C}I_{c}\to V
has a section.
Here,
\xi
is induced by
p_{2}
.
Let
\tau\colon(Y_{c},D_{c})\times V\to(Y,D)\times_{C}V
be a
V
-isomorphism corresponding to
\xi
.
We note that
I_{c}=\mathrm{Isom}_{C}((Y_{c},D_{c})\times C,(Y,D))
.
Take
c^{\prime}\in V
such that
\psi(c^{\prime})=c
.
By replacing
\tau
with the composition
\tau\circ(\tau_{c^{\prime}}\times\mathrm{id}_{V})^{-1}
, we may assume that
\tau_{c^{\prime}}
is the identity.
Furthermore, by replacing
V
with its finite cover, we may assume that any irreducible component of
D_{V}
is a section over
V
.
Thus, for any
v\in V
,
\tau_{v}\colon(Y_{c},D_{c})\to(Y_{v},D_{v})
and irreducible component
E
of
D_{V}
, we may assume that
\tau_{v}
maps
E_{c}
to
E_{v}
.
Therefore,
\tau_{v}
maps
\mathrm{div}(f_{j,c})
and
\mathrm{div}(f_{j,c}+f_{N+1,c})
to
\mathrm{div}(f_{j,v})
and
\mathrm{div}(f_{j,v}+f_{N+1,v})
respectively for any
0\leq j\leq N+1
.
This means
that
q_{c}
and
q_{v}
are isomorphic as log Fano quasimaps by Lemma
3.5
.
This contradicts the assumption that
q
has maximal variation.
We complete the proof.
∎

Let
\pi\colon\mathcal{U}_{Z_{4}}\to Z_{4}
be the canonical morphism, which was defined in Subsection
3.2
.
Then
Z_{4}
admits the CM line bundle
\lambda_{\mathrm{CM},\pi,q}
and this is
PGL(P(1))\times PGL(m+1)
-equivariant.
Thus,
\lambda_{\mathrm{CM},\pi,q}
descends to a line bundle
\lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}}
on
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
.
By the same argument of
[
CP21
, Lemma 10.2]
, we have the following.

Lemma 3.36
.

\lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}}
descends to a
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle
\Lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}}
on
M^{\mathrm{Kps,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
that is uniquely determined up to
\mathbb{Q}
-linear equivalence.

Proof.

First, note that for any closed point
s\in\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
, the stabilizer group
G_{s}
is reductive (by
[
Alp13
, Proposition 12.14]
).
By the proof of Theorem
3.23
,
PGL(m+1)
acts on
Z_{4}
without any non-trivial stabilizer.
Hence, for any closed point
s\in\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
, the stabilizer group
G_{s}
is contained in
PGL(P(1))
.
Here,
G_{s}
is a fiber of

\mathcal{I}:=\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}\times_{\Delta,\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}\times\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota},\Delta}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}

over
s
.
Let
G_{s}^{\circ}
be the identity component of
G_{s}
.
Since
\mathcal{I}
is an Artin stack of finite type, there exists a positive integer
N
such that the order of
G_{s}/G_{s}^{\circ}
divides
N
for any closed point
s
.
Let
l
be a positive integer such that
(\lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}})^{\otimes l}
is a line bundle.
By the same argument of
[
CP21
, Lemma 10.2]
, we see that
G_{s}^{\circ}
acts on
(\lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}})^{\otimes l}_{s}
trivially.
Furthermore,
G_{s}/G_{s}^{\circ}
acts on
(\lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}})^{\otimes Nl}_{s}
trivially.
Thus,
[
Alp13
, Theorem 10.3]
shows that there exists a unique line bundle
L
on
M^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
such that its pullback to
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
coincides with
(\lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}})^{\otimes Nl}
up to isomorphism.
By letting
\Lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}}:=\frac{1}{Nl}L
, we obtain the assertion.
∎

We call
\Lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}}
the
CM line bundle
on
M^{\mathrm{Kps,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
.
The following theorem is the main result of this subsection.

Theorem 3.37
.

Fix
v,u\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
and
r,m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.
We assume
0<u<1-\frac{v}{2}
.
Then, the CM line bundle
\Lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}}
on
M^{\mathrm{Kps,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
is ample.
In particular,
M^{\mathrm{Kps,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
is a projective scheme.

We first show the nefness of the CM line bundle.

Proposition 3.38
.

Let
q\colon(Y,B)\to[\mathrm{Cone}(X)\times C/\mathbb{G}_{m,C}]
be a family of K-semistable log Fano quasimaps of degree
m
and of weight
u
over a smooth proper curve
C
. Let
\pi\colon Y\to C
be the canonical morphism.
Suppose that
0<u<\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathrm{deg}_{Y_{t}}(B_{t})+um\right)
for any general
t\in C
.

Then,
\mathrm{deg}\,\lambda_{\mathrm{CM},\pi,q}\geq 0
.

To show this proposition, we prepare the following lemma, which deals with a special case.

Lemma 3.39
.

Let
q\colon(Y,B)\to[\mathrm{Cone}(X)\times C/\mathbb{G}_{m,C}]
be a family of K-semistable log Fano quasimaps of degree
m
and of weight
u
over a smooth proper curve
C
. Let
\pi\colon Y\to C
be the canonical morphism.
Let
\mathscr{L}
be the line bundle associated with
q
.
Suppose that there exist an effective
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor
D\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathscr{L}
and a family of K-semistable log Fano pairs
\pi^{\prime}\colon(Y^{\prime},B^{\prime})\to C
such that
(Y^{\prime},B^{\prime})|_{U}\cong(Y,B+D)|_{U}
over some non-empty open subset
U
of
C
.

Then,
\mathrm{deg}\,\lambda_{\mathrm{CM},\pi,q}\geq\mathrm{deg}\,\lambda_{\mathrm{CM},\pi^{\prime},(Y^{\prime},B^{\prime})}
.

Proof.

We decompose
D
as
D_{\mathrm{vert}}+D_{\mathrm{hor}}
, where
\pi
maps
D_{\mathrm{vert}}
to finite points on
C
and every irreducible component of
D_{\mathrm{hor}}
is flat over
C
.
Since
D\equiv D_{\mathrm{hor}}+\mu\pi^{-1}(c)
for some
\mu\geq 0
, we have by an easy calculation that

\mathrm{deg}\,\lambda_{\mathrm{CM},\pi,q}\geq\mathrm{deg}\,\lambda_{\mathrm{CM},\pi,(Y,B+D_{\mathrm{hor}})}.

By
[
Hat24b
, Theorem 3.22]
, we obtain that

\mathrm{deg}\,\lambda_{\mathrm{CM},\pi,(Y,B+D_{\mathrm{hor}})}\geq\mathrm{deg}\,\lambda_{\mathrm{CM},\pi^{\prime},(Y^{\prime},B^{\prime})}.

Thus, we obtain the assertion.
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.38
.

Choose a closed point
c\in C
.
By Lemma
3.12
, we can find a general vector
(a_{0},\,\dots,\,a_{N})\in\mathbbm{k}^{N+1}
corresponding to a section
f\in\mathrm{Sec}_{q_{c}}(\mathscr{L}_{c})
such that
(Y_{c},B_{c}+u\mathrm{div}(f_{c}))
is K-semistable.
We decompose
\mathrm{div}(f)
as
D_{\mathrm{vert}}+D_{\mathrm{hor}}
, where
\pi
maps
D_{\mathrm{vert}}
to finite points on
C
and every irreducible component of
D_{\mathrm{hor}}
is flat over
C
.
Since
D_{\mathrm{hor},c}=\mathrm{div}(f_{c})
and
(Y_{c},B_{c}+u\mathrm{div}(f_{c}))
is K-semistable, we see by
[
BLX22
, Corollary 1.2]
and
[
BHLLX21
, Corollary 7.4]
that there exist a finite morphism
g\colon C^{\prime}\to C
from a smooth curve and a family
\pi^{\prime}\colon(Y^{\prime},B^{\prime})\to C^{\prime}
of K-semistable log Fano pairs such that
(Y^{\prime},B^{\prime})|_{U}
and
(Y,B+uD_{\mathrm{hor}})\times_{C}C^{\prime}|_{U}
are isomorphic over some non-empty open subset
U\subset C^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
3.39
, we have

\mathrm{deg}(g)\cdot\mathrm{deg}\,\lambda_{\mathrm{CM},\pi,q}\geq\mathrm{deg}\,\lambda_{\mathrm{CM},\pi^{\prime},(Y^{\prime},B^{\prime})}.

By
[
XZ20
, Corollary 4.7]
, we have

\mathrm{deg}\,\lambda_{\mathrm{CM},\pi^{\prime},(Y^{\prime},B^{\prime})}\geq 0.

We complete the proof.
∎

We quickly recall the nef and bigness for
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundles on proper algebraic spaces.

Definition 3.40
.

Let
X
be a proper algebraic space with a
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle
L
.
We say that
L
is
nef
if for any morphism
\varphi\colon C\to X
from a projective curve,
\mathrm{deg}_{C}(\varphi^{*}L)\geq 0
.
Furthermore, we say that a nef
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle
L
is
big
if for any proper generically finite morphism
\psi\colon V\to X
from a scheme, we have
(\psi^{*}L)^{\mathrm{dim}\,V}>0
.

To show Theorem
3.37
, it suffices to show the following proposition by the Nakai–Moishezon’s criterion for ampleness for algebraic spaces
[
Ko90
, Theorem 3.11]
.

Proposition 3.41
.

Fix
v,u\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
and
r,m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.
We also assume that
0<u<1-\frac{v}{2}
.
Let
S
be an arbitrary proper irreducible and reduced subspace of
M^{\mathrm{Kps,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
. Then,
\Lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}}|_{S}
is big and nef.

For this, we first deal with the following special case.

Lemma 3.42
.

With notation as in Proposition
3.41
, suppose that for any geometric point
\bar{s}\in S
, the K-polystable log Fano quasimap
(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\bar{s}},B)\to[\mathrm{Cone}(X)/\mathbb{G}_{m,\bar{s}}]
corresponding to
\bar{s}
is constant.

Then,
\Lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}}|_{S}
is ample.

Proof.

We define a reduced closed substack
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,const.qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
as the complement of an open substack
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,nonconst.qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
.
By
[
Alp13
, Lemma 4.14]
,
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,const.qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
has the good moduli space
M^{\mathrm{Kps,const.qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
, which is the scheme-theoretic image of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,const.qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
under the canonical morphism
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}\to M^{\mathrm{Kps,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
.
By assumption, we have
S\subset M^{\mathrm{Kps,const.qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
.
Therefore, it suffices to show that
\Lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}}|_{M^{\mathrm{Kps,const.qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}}
is ample.

Let
r^{\prime}
be the denominator of
u
and consider
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss}}_{1,rr^{\prime},v}
the K-moduli stack of log Fano pairs.
We will show that there exists a canonical morphism

\xi\colon\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,const.qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss}}_{1,rr^{\prime},v}\times X.

Take a surjective smooth morphism
S\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,const.qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
and the corresponding object
q\colon(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{B})\to[\mathrm{Cone}(X)\times S/\mathbb{G}_{m,S}]
of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,const.qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}(S)
.
Then, it follows from Lemma
3.12
that there exists a smooth surjection
S^{\prime}\to S
such that for any connected component
S_{i}
of
S^{\prime}
, there exists a general section
f_{i}\in\mathrm{Sec}_{q_{S_{i}}}(\mathscr{L}_{S_{i}})
with which the pair
(\mathcal{C}_{S_{i}},\mathcal{B}_{S_{i}}+u\mathrm{div}(f_{i}))
is a family of K-semistable log pairs.
Since
q_{\bar{s}}
is constant for any geometric point
\bar{s}\in S
, the family
(\mathcal{C}_{S_{i}},\mathcal{B}_{S_{i}}+u\mathrm{div}(f_{i}))
is independent from the choice of
f_{i}
.
This shows that
(\mathcal{C}_{S_{i}},\mathcal{B}_{S_{i}}+u\mathrm{div}(f_{i}))
over
S_{i}
defines a descent datum and this descends to an object of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss}}_{1,rr^{\prime},v}(S)
(for the definition of descent data, see
[
HaHa25
, Remark 2.10]
).
On the other hand, by replacing
S_{i}
with an étale covering, we may assume that there exists a section
\mu_{i}\colon S_{i}\to\mathcal{C}_{S_{i}}
for any
i
and we can take a morphism
\theta_{i}=q_{S_{i}}\circ\mu_{i}
since
\mathcal{C}\to S
is smooth.
Since
q_{\bar{s}}
is constant for any geometric point
\bar{s}\in S
, we see that
q_{S_{i}}
factors through an open subscheme
X\times S\cong[\mathrm{Cone}(X)\setminus\{0\}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]\times S\subset[\mathrm{Cone}(X)\times S/\mathbb{G}_{m,S}]
and
\theta_{i}
is determined independently from the choice of
\mu_{i}
since
S
is reduced.
Therefore,
\{\theta_{i}\}_{i}
defines an effective descent datum and this descends to a morphism
\theta\colon S\to X
.
It is easy to see that
\theta
descends to a canonical morphism
\theta^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,const.qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}\to X
.
By using
\theta^{\prime}
, we obtain the desired morphism
\xi
.

By
[
Alp13
, Theorem 6.6]
,
\xi
induces a map of algebraic spaces

\xi^{\prime}\colon M^{\mathrm{Kps,const.qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}\to M^{\mathrm{Kps}}_{1,rr^{\prime},v}\times X.

By comparing
\mathbbm{k}
-valued points of them, we see that
\xi^{\prime}
is a quasi-finite morphism.
This fact and
[
Ols16
, Theorem 7.2.10]
show that
M^{\mathrm{Kps,const.qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
is a scheme.
Since
M^{\mathrm{Kps,const.qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
and
M^{\mathrm{Kps}}_{1,rr^{\prime},v}\times X
are proper,
\xi^{\prime}
is also finite.
Take an arbitrary morphism
g\colon C\to M^{\mathrm{Kps,const.qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
.
Let
\Lambda_{\mathrm{CM}}
be the CM line bundle on
M^{\mathrm{Kps}}_{1,rr^{\prime},v}
, which is ample by
[
XZ20
, Theorem 7.9]
.
Let
p_{1}\colon M^{\mathrm{Kps}}_{1,rr^{\prime},v}\times X\to M^{\mathrm{Kps}}_{1,rr^{\prime},v}
and
p_{2}\colon M^{\mathrm{Kps}}_{1,rr^{\prime},v}\times X\to X
be the projections.
To show the ampleness of
\Lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}}|_{M^{\mathrm{Kps,const.qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}}
, it suffices to show that

\mathrm{deg}g^{*}\xi^{\prime*}(p_{1}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{CM}}+2vp_{2}^{*}\iota^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}(1))=\mathrm{deg}g^{*}\Lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}},

which shows that
\xi^{\prime*}(p_{1}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{CM}}+2vp_{2}^{*}\iota^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}(1))\equiv\Lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}}|_{M^{\mathrm{Kps,const.qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}}
.

By Lemma
2.24
and replacing
C
with its finite cover, we may assume that
g
lifts up to
\tilde{g}\colon C\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,const.qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
.
Take the family
q\colon(Y,B)\to[\mathrm{Cone}(X)\times C/\mathbb{G}_{m,C}]
of K-semistable log Fano quasimaps over
C
corresponding to
\tilde{g}
.
Let
\mathscr{L}
be the line bundle induced by
q
and take a general section
f\in\mathrm{Sec}_{q}(\mathscr{L})
such that the support of
\mathrm{div}(f)
does not contain all fibers of
\pi\colon Y\to C
.
We decompose
u\mathrm{div}(f)
as
D_{\mathrm{hor}}+D_{\mathrm{vert}}
into the horizontal part and the vertical part.
Note that
D_{\mathrm{vert}}
is numerically equivalent to
\mu F
, where
\mu\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
and
F
is a fiber of
\pi
.
Under
\xi
,
q
assigns a morphism
\psi\colon C\to X
and the family
(Y,B+D_{\mathrm{hor}})
of K-semistable log Fano pairs over
C
.
Since we can regard
q
is a family of constant maps from
Y
to
X
over
C
and
\pi
is a contraction, we see that the line bundle
\mathscr{L}-u^{-1}D_{\mathrm{hor}}
defines a morphism
q=\psi\circ\pi\colon Y\to X
and hence
u\mathrm{deg}\psi^{*}\iota^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}(1)=\mu
.
In this case,

\displaystyle\mathrm{deg}g^{*}\Lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}}

\displaystyle=(-(K_{Y/C}+B+u\mathscr{L}))^{2}

\displaystyle=(-(K_{Y/C}+B+D_{\mathrm{hor}}))^{2}+2v\mu

\displaystyle=\mathrm{deg}g^{*}\xi^{\prime*}(p_{1}^{*}\Lambda_{\mathrm{CM}}+2vp_{2}^{*}\iota^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}(1)).

Thus, we complete the proof.
∎

Lemma 3.43
.

With notation as in Proposition
3.41
, suppose that the geometric generic point
\bar{\eta}\in S
corresponds to a K-polystable non constant log Fano quasimap.

Then,
\Lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}}|_{S}
is big and nef.

Proof.

By Chow’s lemma for algebraic spaces (cf.
[
Ols16
, Theorem 7.4.1]
), there exists a projective smooth variety
T
and a generically finite and proper morphism
g\colon T\to S
.
By Lemma
2.24
and replacing
T
with a proper generically finite cover of
T
, we may assume that there exist an open subscheme
T^{\circ}\subset T
and a lift
h\colon T^{\circ}\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}\times_{M^{\mathrm{Kps,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}}S
of
g|_{T^{\circ}}
such that for any point
t\in T^{\circ}
,
h(t)
is the unique closed point of
\mathrm{Spec}\,\kappa(g(t))\times_{M^{\mathrm{Kps,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
.
Fix an ample line bundle
H
on
T
.
Now, it suffices to show that
g^{*}\Lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}}
is big since
\Lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}}
is nef by Proposition
3.38
.

Let
\eta_{T}
be the generic point of
T
and
q^{\circ}\colon(Y^{\circ},B^{\circ})\to[\mathrm{Cone}(X)\times T^{\circ}/\mathbb{G}_{m,T^{\circ}}]
the family of K-polystable log Fano quasimaps corresponding to
h
.
Let
\mathscr{L}^{\circ}
be the line bundle on
Y^{\circ}
associated with
q^{\circ}
.
Since
q^{\circ}_{\overline{\eta_{T}}}
is non-constant by the construction of
h
, there exist
N+2
sections
f_{0},f_{1},\ldots,f_{N+1}\in\mathrm{Sec}_{q^{\circ}}(\mathscr{L}^{\circ})\setminus\{0\}
such that
\mathrm{div}(f_{i,\overline{\eta_{T}}})\neq\mathrm{div}(f_{N+1,\overline{\eta_{T}}})
and
f_{0},\ldots,f_{N}
correspond to a basis of
\mathbbm{k}^{N+1}
.
By shrinking
T^{\circ}
if necessary, we may assume that any
\mathrm{div}(f_{i})
or
\mathrm{div}(f_{i}+f_{N+1})
does not contain
Y^{\circ}_{t}
for any
t\in T^{\circ}
.
By applying Lemma
3.35
, the family of pairs
(Y^{\circ},B^{\circ}+\sum_{j=0}^{N+1}\mathrm{div}(f_{j})+\sum_{j=0}^{N}(\mathrm{div}(f_{j}+f_{N+1})))
has maximal variation.
Furthermore, by choosing
f_{j}
general enough, we may assume that the pair

\left(Y^{\circ}_{\overline{\eta_{T}}},B^{\circ}_{\overline{\eta_{T}}}+\frac{u}{2(N+2)}\sum^{N+1}_{j=0}\mathrm{div}(f_{j,\overline{\eta_{T}}})+\frac{u}{2(N+1)}\sum^{N}_{j=0}(\mathrm{div}(f_{j,\overline{\eta_{T}}}+f_{N+1,\overline{\eta_{T}}}))\right)

is K-stable.
Indeed, since
q^{\circ}_{\overline{\eta_{T}}}
is K-stable, this follows from Lemma
3.12
.
Furthermore, by shrinking
T^{\circ}
if necessary, we may assume that

\pi^{\circ}\colon\left(Y^{\circ},B^{\circ}+\frac{u}{2(N+2)}\sum^{N+1}_{j=0}\mathrm{div}(f_{j})+\frac{u}{2(N+1)}\sum^{N}_{j=0}(\mathrm{div}(f_{j}+f_{N+1}))\right)\to T^{\circ}

is a family of K-stable log Fano pairs with maximal variation (
[
BL22
, Theorem A]
).
We note that all fibers of
\pi^{\circ}
belongs to the collection of objects of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss}}_{1,2N(N+1)r,v}
.

For any strongly movable curve
C\subset T
(for the definition, see
[
BDPP13
, Definition 1.3]
), we take the canonical morphism
\varphi\colon\tilde{C}\to T
from the normalization
\tilde{C}
of
C
.
By the definition of movable curves, it is easy to see that there exist a projective morphism of smooth quasi-projective varieties
\gamma\colon U\to V
such that every geometric fiber of
r
is a connected smooth curve and a dominant morphism
\mu\colon U\to T
such that
\mu|{U_{p}}\colon U_{p}\to T
coincides with
\varphi\colon\tilde{C}\to T
for some closed point
p\in V
.
Taking hyperplane sections of
V
, we may assume that
\mu
is generically finite.
By Lemma
2.24
and
[
AHLH23
, Theorem A.8]
applied to
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss}}_{1,2N(N+1)r,v}
, taking a non-empty open subset
W\subset V
and a finite cover
\beta\colon\tilde{U}\to U\times_{V}W
such that
\tilde{U}\to W
is smooth with connected fibers, we may assume that there exists a family

\tilde{\pi}\colon(\tilde{Y},\tilde{B})\to\tilde{U}

of K-polystable log Fano pairs such that

\displaystyle(\tilde{Y},\tilde{B})|_{(\mu\circ\beta)^{-1}(T^{\circ})}\cong

\displaystyle\left(Y^{\circ},B^{\circ}+\frac{u}{2(N+2)}\sum^{N+1}_{j=0}\mathrm{div}(f_{j})+\frac{u}{2(N+1)}\sum^{N}_{j=0}(\mathrm{div}(f_{j}+f_{N+1}))\right)\times_{T^{\circ}}(\mu\circ\beta)^{-1}(T^{\circ}).

Here, we note that
W
might not contain
p
any longer.

On the other hand, let
H
be an ample line bundle on
T
.
Take a sufficiently large and divisible
M\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and a positive integer
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
such that the following conditions hold (cf.
[
XZ20
, Definition 6.1]
) for any scheme
S^{\prime}
and
f^{\prime}\colon(Y^{\prime},B^{\prime})\to S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss}}_{1,2N(N+1)r,v}(S^{\prime})
.

(1)

L^{\prime}:=M(-K_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}-B^{\prime})
is relatively
\pi^{\circ}
-very ample line bundle,

(2)

if
D^{\prime}:=\mathrm{Supp}(B^{\prime}),

then
H^{j}(Y^{\prime}_{s},\mathcal{O}_{Y^{\prime}_{s}}(mL^{\prime}_{s}))=H^{j}(D^{\prime}_{s},\mathcal{O}_{D^{\prime}_{s}}(mL_{s}^{\prime}|_{D^{\prime}_{s}}))=0
for any
s\in S^{\prime}
,
m>0
and
j>0
,

(3)

for any closed point
s\in S^{\prime}
, the embeddings of
D^{\prime}_{s}
and
Y^{\prime}_{s}
into
\mathbb{P}^{h^{0}(Y^{\prime}_{s},\mathcal{O}_{Y^{\prime}_{s}}(L^{\prime}_{s}))-1}
defined by
L^{\prime}_{s}
are cut out by homogeneous polynomials of at most degree
d
at least set theoretically,

(4)

for any closed point
s\in S^{\prime}
,
\mathrm{Sym}^{d}H^{0}(Y^{\prime}_{s},\mathcal{O}_{Y^{\prime}_{s}}(L^{\prime}_{s}))\to H^{0}(Y^{\prime}_{s},\mathcal{O}_{Y^{\prime}_{s}}(dL^{\prime}_{s}))
and
H^{0}(Y^{\prime}_{s},\mathcal{O}_{Y^{\prime}_{s}}(dL^{\prime}_{s}))\to H^{0}(D^{\prime}_{s},\mathcal{O}_{D^{\prime}_{s}}(dL^{\prime}_{s}|_{D^{\prime}_{s}}))
are surjective.

Indeed, we can take such
M
and
d
by the fact that
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss}}_{1,2N(N+1)r,v}
is of finite type over
\mathbbm{k}
(cf. Theorem
2.56
).
We take
r^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
such that
r^{\prime}\lambda_{\mathrm{CM},f^{\prime}}
is a line bundle for any
f^{\prime}
.
By
[
XZ20
, Theorem 7.2]
, there exists
c_{0}>0
depending only on the choice of
H
,
r^{\prime}
and
d
but independent from the choice of
\tilde{C}
and
U
such that

\mathrm{deg}(\lambda_{\mathrm{CM},\tilde{\pi},(\tilde{Y},\tilde{B})}|_{\tilde{U}_{p^{\prime}}})\geq c_{0}\mathrm{deg}(\beta^{*}\mu^{*}H|_{\tilde{U}_{p^{\prime}}})

for any general point
p^{\prime}\in W
.

In this paragraph, we show the following inequality

\mathrm{deg}(\beta^{*}\mu^{*}g^{*}\Lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}}|_{\tilde{U}_{p^{\prime}}})\geq\mathrm{deg}(\lambda_{\mathrm{CM},\tilde{\pi},(\tilde{Y},\tilde{B})}|_{\tilde{U}_{p^{\prime}}})

for any general point
p^{\prime}\in W
.

By
[
AHLH23
, Theorem A.8]
, after possibly replacing
\tilde{U}_{p^{\prime}}
with its finite cover, we may assume that there exist
a family of K-polystable log Fano quasimaps

q_{2}\colon(Y_{2},B_{2})\to[\mathrm{Cone}(X)\times\tilde{U}_{p^{\prime}}/\mathbb{G}_{m,\tilde{U}_{p^{\prime}}}]

such that
q_{2}|_{(\mu\circ\beta)^{-1}(T^{\circ})\cap\tilde{U}_{p^{\prime}}}
coincides with
q^{\circ}_{(\mu\circ\beta)^{-1}(T^{\circ})\cap\tilde{U}_{p^{\prime}}}
.
Then, Lemma
3.39
shows the desired inequality.
Indeed, the CM line bundle of
q_{2}
coincides with
\beta^{*}\mu^{*}g^{*}\Lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}}|_{\tilde{U}_{p^{\prime}}}
in this case.

Finally, since
\mathrm{deg}_{U_{p^{\prime}}}(\mu^{*}g^{*}\Lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}}|_{U_{p^{\prime}}})=\mathrm{deg}_{\tilde{C}}(\mu^{*}g^{*}\Lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}}|_{\tilde{C}})
and
\mathrm{deg}_{U_{p^{\prime}}}(\mu^{*}H|_{U_{p^{\prime}}})=\mathrm{deg}_{\tilde{C}}(\mu^{*}H|_{\tilde{C}})
, we have the inequality

\mathrm{deg}_{\tilde{C}}(\mu^{*}g^{*}\Lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}}|_{\tilde{C}})\geq c_{0}\cdot\mathrm{deg}_{\tilde{C}}(\mu^{*}H|_{\tilde{C}}).

This inequality holds for any strongly movable curve
C\subset T
and we see that
g^{*}\Lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}}
is big by
[
BDPP13
, Theorem 2.2]
since
c_{0}
does not depend on the choice of
C
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.41
.

This immediately follows from Lemmas
3.42
and
3.43
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.37
.

This immediately follows from Proposition
3.41
and
[
Ko90
, Theorem 3.11]
.
∎

3.6.
K-moduli theory for an arbitrary weight

As we saw, we established the K-moduli theory for log Fano quasimaps when the weight is sufficiently small.
From now, we extend this theory to an arbitrary weight case.

Fix
v,u\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
and
r,m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.
Take an arbitrary
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and consider the
l
-th Veronese embedding
v_{l}\colon\mathbb{P}^{N}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}
, where
N^{\prime}:=\binom{N+l}{l}-1
.
Let
\mathrm{Cone}^{\prime}(X)\subset\mathbb{A}^{N^{\prime}+1}
be the affine cone with respect to the embedding
v_{l}\circ\iota\colon X\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}
.
By construction, there is a canonical morphism
v^{\prime}_{l}\colon\mathrm{Cone}(X)\to\mathrm{Cone}^{\prime}(X)
.
Note that
v^{\prime}_{l}
is not
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant in the natural way but if we consider the composition of the natural action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
on
\mathrm{Cone}^{\prime}(X)
and

\mathbb{G}_{m}\ni t\mapsto t^{l}\in\mathbb{G}_{m}

instead of the canonical action, then
v^{\prime}_{l}
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant.
Therefore, we obtain the canonical morphism of stacks
[v_{l}]\colon[\mathrm{Cone}(X)/\mathbb{G}_{m}]\to[\mathrm{Cone}^{\prime}(X)/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
induced by
v^{\prime}_{l}
, where
[\mathrm{Cone}^{\prime}(X)/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
is the quotient stack with respect to the canonical action.
By using this morphism, we can obtain the natural morphism of stacks

\tilde{v}_{l}\colon\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{lm,r,\frac{u}{l},v,v_{l}\circ\iota},

which maps an arbitrary object
q\colon(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{B})\to[\mathrm{Cone}(X)\times S/\mathbb{G}_{m,S}]
of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}(S)
to
[v_{l}]\circ q\colon(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{B})\to[\mathrm{Cone}(X)\times S/\mathbb{G}_{m,S}]
as an object of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{lm,r,\frac{u}{l},v,v_{l}\circ\iota}(S)
.
Indeed, if
\mathscr{L}
is the line bundle associated with
q
, then we can obtain a family of quasimaps
[v_{l}]\circ q
such that
\mathscr{L}^{\otimes l}
is associated with
[v_{l}]\circ q
.
Moreover, we can see that the fixed part of
[v_{l}]\circ q
coincides with the fixed part of
q
.
By this, it is easy to see that
q
is K-(semi)stable if and only if so is
[v_{l}]\circ q
(cf. Remark
3.13
).
Therefore, we can construct
\tilde{v}_{l}
as claimed.
Similarly, we can easily deduce that
q
is K-polystable if and only if so is
[v_{l}]\circ q
since we know that if
q
is K-polystable but not K-stable then
q
is constant.

Lemma 3.44
.

Fix

v,u\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
and
r,m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.
Take an arbitrary
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and consider the
l
-th Veronese embedding
v_{l}\colon\mathbb{P}^{N}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}
, where
N^{\prime}:=\binom{N+l}{l}-1
.

Then,
\tilde{v}_{l}\colon\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{lm,r,\frac{u}{l},v,v_{l}\circ\iota}
defined as above is a closed immersion.

Proof.

Let
q^{\prime}\colon(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{B})\to[\mathrm{Cone}^{\prime}(X)\times S/\mathbb{G}_{m,S}]
be an object of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{lm,r,\frac{u}{l},v,v_{l}\circ\iota}(S)
, where
\mathrm{Cone}^{\prime}(X)\subset\mathbb{A}^{N^{\prime}+1}
is the affine cone with respect to the embedding
v_{l}\circ\iota\colon X\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}
.
To show this lemma, it suffices to show in the above situation that there exists a closed subscheme
S^{\prime}\subset S
such that for any morphism
g\colon T\to S
from a scheme, then
g
factors through
S^{\prime}
if and only if there exists a unique object
q\in\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}(T)
such that
q^{\prime}_{T}\cong\tilde{v}_{l}(q)
.
We note that if
S^{\prime}
exists, then such
S^{\prime}
is unique by the property as we stated above.
From now, we will show the existence of such
S^{\prime}
.
Let
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}
be the line bundle associated with
q^{\prime}
.
Take an arbitrary point
s_{0}\in S
.
Let
e_{0},\ldots,e_{N}
be the canonical coordinates of
\mathbb{A}^{N+1}_{\mathbbm{k}}
.
Suppose that there exists a suitable choice of coordinates
e^{\prime}_{0},\ldots,e^{\prime}_{N^{\prime}}
of
\mathbb{A}^{N^{\prime}+1}_{\mathbbm{k}}
such that
e^{\prime}_{0},\ldots,e^{\prime}_{N}
are the images of
e_{0}^{l},\ldots,e_{N}^{l}
via the Veronese embedding.
Since the assertion is local, we may shrink
S
and assume that
\mathcal{C}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}_{S}
.
Let
f^{\prime}_{0},\ldots,f^{\prime}_{N^{\prime}}
be the canonical sections corresponding to
e^{\prime}_{0},\ldots,e^{\prime}_{N^{\prime}}
.
Then, we see that for any
s\in S
, some
f^{\prime}_{i}
for
0\leq i\leq N
satisfies that
f^{\prime}_{i,s}\not=0
since
q^{\prime}
is a quasimap to
[\mathrm{Cone}^{\prime}(X)\times S/\mathbb{G}_{m,S}]
and
X\subset\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}
is contained in the image of the Veronese embedding
v_{l}
.
We may assume that
f^{\prime}_{0,s}\not=0
for any
s\in S
by shrinking
S
and changing the numbering.
By
\mathrm{div}(f^{\prime}_{0,s})
, we can take the corresponding morphism
\mu\colon S\to\mathbf{Div}^{lm}_{\mathcal{C}/S}
, where
\mathbf{Div}^{lm}_{\mathcal{C}/S}
denotes the open and closed subscheme of
\mathbf{Div}_{\mathcal{C}/S}
parametrizing relative Cartier divisors of degree
lm
.

Here, we note that
\mathbf{Div}^{d}_{\mathcal{C}/S}
is isomorphic to the
d
-th symmetric product of
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{S}
over
S
for any integer
d>0
(cf.
[
F+05
, Remark 9.3.9]
).
Now, we will show that the natural morphism
\xi\colon\mathbf{Div}^{m}_{\mathcal{C}/S}\to\mathbf{Div}^{lm}_{\mathcal{C}/S}
, which is induced by multiplying
l
to divisors, is a closed immersion in this case.
Since
\mathbf{Div}^{m}_{\mathcal{C}/S}
and
\mathbf{Div}^{lm}_{\mathcal{C}/S}
are projective over
S
, we may assume that
S=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbbm{k}
by
[
GW10
, Proposition 12.93]
.
It is well-known that the
d
-th symmetric product
(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{(d)}\cong\mathbb{P}^{d}
as
[
F+05
, Example 7.1.3 (1)]
.
Fix a natural open immersion
\mathbb{A}^{1}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
For any point
p\in\mathbf{Div}^{m}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}/\mathbbm{k}}\cong\mathbb{P}^{m}
, by changing the projective coordinate of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
, we may assume that
p\in(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{(m)}\subset\mathbf{Div}^{m}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}/\mathbbm{k}}
.
Let
\pi_{d}\colon\mathbb{A}^{d}\to(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{(d)}\cong\mathbb{A}^{d}/\mathfrak{S}_{d}
be the canonical morphism of the geometric quotient, where
\mathfrak{S}_{d}
is the
d
-th symmetric group for any
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.
Then,
\xi|_{(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{(m)}}
is induced by the composition of a morphism
\mathbb{A}^{m}\to\mathbb{A}^{lm}
that is the
m
-th product of the diagonal morphism
\Delta\colon\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\mathbb{A}^{l}
and the canonical morphism
\pi_{lm}\colon\mathbb{A}^{lm}\to(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{(lm)}
.
It is easy to check that
\xi|_{(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{(m)}}
is a closed immersion.
Indeed, let
\xi^{\sharp}\colon\mathbbm{k}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{ml}]^{(\mathfrak{S}_{ml})}\to\mathbbm{k}[y_{1},\ldots,y_{m}]^{(\mathfrak{S}_{m})}
be the morphism of invariant rings corresponding to
\xi|_{(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{(m)}}
.
Then,
\mathrm{Im}(\xi^{\sharp})
contains
y_{1}+\ldots+y_{m}
.
By induction, we can show that
\mathrm{Im}(\xi^{\sharp})
contains the elementary symmetric polynomials of degree
1\leq k\leq m
.
Indeed, we may assume that
\mathrm{Im}(\xi^{\sharp})
contains all elementary symmetric polynomials of degree at most
k
.
Then,
(y_{1}+\ldots+y_{m})^{k+1}-(y_{1}^{k+1}+\ldots+y_{m}^{k+1})
is generated by elementary symmetric polynomials of degree at most
k+1
.
Thus, it is easy to see that
\mathrm{Im}(\xi^{\sharp})
also contains the elementary symmetric polynomial of degree
k+1
.
Therefore,
\xi^{\sharp}
is surjective.
Since
\xi
is proper and injective, we see that
\xi
is a closed immersion.

Finally, set
S^{\prime}:=\mu^{-1}(\xi(\mathbf{Div}_{\mathcal{C}/S}^{m}))
and we will check that this
S^{\prime}
is the desired closed subscheme.
Indeed, there exists a unique relative Cartier divisor
\mathscr{D}
over
S^{\prime}
such that
l\mathscr{D}=\mathrm{div}(f^{\prime}_{0})_{S^{\prime}}
.
Let
\mathscr{L}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathscr{D})
.
Then, we have that
\mathscr{L}^{\otimes l}\sim\mathscr{L}^{\prime}
.
By
H^{1}(\mathcal{C}_{s},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}_{s}}(\mathscr{D}_{s}))=0
for any
s\in S
, we can choose a section
f_{0}\in H^{0}(\mathcal{C},\mathscr{L})
by shrinking
S
if necessary.
Then, there exists a section
c\in H^{0}(S,\mathcal{O}_{S}^{\times})
such that
f_{0}^{l}=cf^{\prime}_{0}
.
Let
g^{\prime}_{ij}\in\mathrm{Sec}_{q^{\prime}}(\mathscr{L})
be the sections corresponding to the sections of
H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N^{\prime}}}(1))
, which is mapped to
e_{0}^{l-j}\cdot e^{j}_{i}
under the Veronese embedding
v_{l}
.
Then, we can set
f_{i}:=\frac{g^{\prime}_{i1}}{f^{\prime}_{0}}f_{0}
as well-defined sections in
H^{0}(\mathcal{C},\mathscr{L})
.
Indeed,
\mathrm{div}\left(\frac{(g^{\prime}_{i1})^{l}}{(f^{\prime}_{0})^{l}}\right)+\mathrm{div}(f^{\prime}_{0})
is an effective relative Cartier divisor linearly equivalent to
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}
since

\frac{g^{\prime}_{0j}}{g^{\prime}_{1j}}=\frac{g^{\prime}_{1j}}{g^{\prime}_{2j}}=\ldots=\frac{g^{\prime}_{l-1j}}{g^{\prime}_{lj}}

by the property of
v_{l}
, where
g^{\prime}_{0j}=f^{\prime}_{0}
.
By using the functions
f_{0},\ldots,f_{N}
, we can define the quasimap structure
q\colon\mathcal{C}_{S^{\prime}}\to[\mathrm{Cone}(X)\times S^{\prime}/\mathbb{G}_{m,S^{\prime}}]
.
Furthermore, we see that
q\colon(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{B})\to[\mathrm{Cone}(X)\times S^{\prime}/\mathbb{G}_{m,S^{\prime}}]
is a family of K-semistable log Fano quasimaps and
q^{\prime}_{S^{\prime}}=[v_{l}]\circ q
.
It is easy to check the uniqueness of such
q
.
On the other hand, let
g\colon T\to S
be a morphism from a scheme such that there exists an object
q\in\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}(T)
such that
q^{\prime}_{T}\cong\tilde{v}_{l}(q)
up to isomorphism.
By the property of
q
, if we let
\mathscr{L}
be the associated line bundle with
q
, then there exists a section
f\in\mathrm{Sec}_{q}(\mathscr{L})
such that
l\mathrm{div}(f)=\mathrm{div}(f^{\prime}_{0})
.
Then, we see that
g
factors through
S^{\prime}
by the property of
S^{\prime}
.
Thus, we obtain the assertion.
∎

Theorem 3.45
.

Fix a closed embedding of a projective normal variety
\iota\colon X\subset\mathbb{P}^{N}
,
v,u\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
and
r,m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.
Then,
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
is an Artin stack of finite type over
\mathbbm{k}
with a good moduli space
M^{\mathrm{Kps,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
that is projective and the set of
\mathbbm{k}
-valued points of
M^{\mathrm{Kps,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
naturally corresponds to the set of isomorphic classes of the K-polystable quasimaps bijectively.
Furthermore,
\Lambda^{\mathrm{qmaps}}_{\mathrm{CM}}
is ample on
M^{\mathrm{Kps,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
.

Proof.

If
u<1-\frac{v}{2}
, the assertion follows from Theorems
3.23
,
3.28
,
3.31
and
3.37
.
We deal with the general case.
By Lemma
3.44
, take a sufficiently large integer
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
such that
\tilde{v}_{l}\colon\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{lm,r,\frac{u}{l},v,v_{l}\circ\iota}
is a closed immersion and
\frac{u}{l}<1-\frac{v}{2}
.
Then
[
Alp13
, Lemma 4.14]
shows the assertion since
\tilde{v}_{l}^{*}\lambda_{\mathrm{CM}}^{\mathrm{qmaps}}
coincides with the CM line bundle defined on
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kss,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
.
∎

Finally, we remark that
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kst,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
is a separated Deligne–Mumford stack.
Indeed, to see this, we may assume that
u<1-\frac{v}{2}
by Lemma
3.44
.
In this case, it is easy to see that the natural morphism
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kst,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}\to M^{\mathrm{Kps,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
is
\mathsf{S}
-complete by Lemmas
3.19
,
3.20
and
[
AHLH23
, Proposition 3.43 (3)]
.
Thus, Corollary
3.25
and
[
AHLH23
, Proposition 3.46]
show that
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kst,qmaps}}_{m,r,u,v,\iota}
is separated.

4.
Morphism between two moduli

Throughout this section, we work over
\mathbb{C}
.
Fix
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and
v\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.
We consider
\mathcal{M}_{d-1,v}^{\mathrm{klt},\mathrm{CY}}
,
\mathcal{M}_{d-1,v}^{\mathrm{Ab}}
, and
\mathcal{M}_{d-1,v}^{\mathrm{symp}}
.
Note that
\mathcal{M}_{d-1,v}^{\mathrm{Ab}}\sqcup\mathcal{M}_{d-1,v}^{\mathrm{symp}}
is an open and closed smooth substack of
\mathcal{M}_{d-1,v}^{\mathrm{klt},\mathrm{CY}}
(cf. Lemma
2.30
and Corollary
2.45
).
Let
\mathcal{V}
be an arbitrary irreducible component and
V
the coarse moduli space.
We have seen that
V
admits the Baily–Borel compactification
\overline{V}^{\mathrm{BB}}
.

We consider a substack
\mathcal{M}_{d,v,u,r,V}^{\mathrm{CYFib}}\subset\mathcal{M}_{d,v,u,r}
such that for any scheme
S
, an object
f\colon(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{A})\to\mathcal{C}\in\mathcal{M}_{d,v,u,r}(S)
is an object of
\mathcal{M}_{d,v,u,r,V}^{\mathrm{CYFib}}(S)
if and only if for any point
s\in S
with the generic point
\eta
of
\mathcal{C}_{s}
, we have
(\mathcal{X}_{\overline{\eta}},\mathcal{L}_{\overline{\eta}})\in\mathcal{V}(\mathrm{Spec}\,\overline{\kappa(\eta)})
.
Then
\mathcal{M}_{d,v,u,r,V}^{\mathrm{CYFib}}
is an open and closed substack of
\mathcal{M}_{d,v,u,r}
since
\mathcal{V}
is a connected component of
\mathcal{M}_{d-1,v}^{\mathrm{klt,CY}}
.
Since
\mathcal{M}_{d,v,u,r,V}^{\mathrm{CYFib}}
is a separated Deligne–Mumford stack of finite type, there exists a coarse moduli space
M_{d,v,u,r,V}^{\mathrm{CYFib}}
.
Let

\nu\colon(\mathcal{M}_{d,v,u,V}^{\mathrm{CYFib}})^{\nu}\to\mathcal{M}_{d,v,u,r,V}^{\mathrm{CYFib}}

be the normalization.
We omit
r
here because the normalization of
\mathcal{M}_{d,v,u,r,V}^{\mathrm{CYFib}}
does not depend on the choice of
r
as we noted in Definition
2.57
.
Let
(M_{d,v,u,V}^{\mathrm{CYFib}})^{\nu}
denote the coarse moduli space of
(\mathcal{M}_{d,v,u,V}^{\mathrm{CYFib}})^{\nu}
.

In this section, we discuss the positivity of the CM line bundle
\Lambda_{\mathrm{CM},t}|_{M_{d,v,u,r,V}^{\mathrm{CYFib}}}
for any sufficiently large
t>0
in the situation of Definition
2.65
.

Setup 4.1
.

Set
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
such that
k
is the smallest positive integer among
l
such that there exist finitely many integral automorphic forms of weight
l
that induce a closed immersion
V\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{L}
for some
L\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.
If
\mathcal{V}
generically parametrizes irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifolds with the second Betti number
b_{2}\geq 5
, then we set
g:=b_{2}-3
and
d^{\prime}:=\frac{d-1}{2}
.
Otherwise, we set
g:=d
and
d^{\prime}=1
.
Note that
\Lambda_{\mathrm{Hodge}}^{\otimes gk}
(see Definition
2.54
) is a very ample line bundle on
\overline{V}^{\mathrm{BB}}
by Theorem
2.52
.

We will use the notation in Setup
4.1
throughout this section.
Theorem
4.2
is necessary to correspond a family of uniformly adiabatically K-stable klt–trivial fibrations over curves to a family of quasimaps.

Theorem 4.2
.

We put
l=gkl_{0}
, where
l_{0}
as in Lemma
2.62
.
Note that
l
is a positive integer depending only on
d
,
v
,
u
,
r
, and
V
.
Then the the following holds.
Let
S
be a smooth affine variety and
f\colon(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{A})\to\mathcal{P}
a family belonging to
\mathcal{M}_{d,v,u,r,V}^{\mathrm{CYFib}}(S)
such that
\mathcal{A}
is represented by a line bundle on
\mathcal{X}
.
Let
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{klt}}
be the largest open subset of
\mathcal{P}
such that all geometric fibers of
f
over
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{klt}}
are klt.
Let
\mu^{\circ}\colon\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{klt}}\to V
be the morphism induced by the family
f|_{f^{-1}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{klt}})}
of klt Calabi–Yau varieties.
Take an Ambro model
\rho\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}\to\mathcal{P}
with respect to
f
, and let
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
be the moduli
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor and the discriminant
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor on
\mathcal{P}
, respectively.
Then, after replacing
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}
with a higher birational model suitably, we have:

(1)

l\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}
and
l\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
are both
\mathbb{Z}
-divisors, and

(2)

putting
L:=h^{0}(\overline{V}^{\mathrm{BB}},\Lambda^{\otimes l}_{\mathrm{Hodge}})-1
, then there exist
L+1
sections

\widetilde{\varphi}_{0},\ldots,\widetilde{\varphi}_{L}\in H^{0}(\widetilde{\mathcal{P}},\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}}(l\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}))

that generate
l\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}
and define a morphism
\widetilde{\mu}\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}\to\mathbb{P}^{L}
such that
\widetilde{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{O}(1)\sim l\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}
,
\widetilde{\mu}
factors
\iota\colon\overline{V}^{\mathrm{BB}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{L}
and this is equivalent to the composition of
\rho
,
\mu^{\circ}
and
\iota
as rational maps.
Here,
\iota\colon\overline{V}^{\mathrm{BB}}\to\mathbb{P}^{L}
is induced by the complete linear system
|\Lambda^{\otimes l}_{\mathrm{Hodge}}|

=|\iota^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{L}}(d^{\prime}l_{0})|
.

Proof.

Since
S
is a smooth affine variety and
f\colon(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{A})\to\mathcal{P}
is in particular an object of
\mathcal{M}_{d,v,u,r}
, we see that
\mathcal{P}
is a smooth quasi-projective variety and
K_{\mathcal{X}}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier.
By our assumption on
\mathcal{A}
, we may regard
\mathcal{A}
as a Cartier divisor on
\mathcal{X}
.
Let
\mathcal{P}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}^{c}
be an open immersion to a normal projective variety
\mathcal{P}^{c}
.
By applying Corollary
2.11
to
f\colon(\mathcal{X},0)\to\mathcal{P}
,
\mathcal{A}
, and
\mathcal{P}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}^{c}
, we get a diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}^{c}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{c}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}^{c}}

and an
f^{c}
-ample
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor
\mathcal{A}^{c}
on
\mathcal{X}^{c}
such that
f^{c}\colon(\mathcal{X}^{c},0)\to\mathcal{P}^{c}
is a klt-trivial fibration and
\mathcal{A}^{c}|_{\mathcal{X}}=\mathcal{A}
.
Now we may apply Theorem
2.52
to the polarized klt-trivial fibration
f^{c}\colon(\mathcal{X}^{c},\mathcal{A}^{c})\to\mathcal{P}^{c}
and a projective birational morphism
\widetilde{\rho}\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}^{c}\to\mathcal{P}^{c}
from an Ambro model
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}^{c}
.
By fixing
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}^{c}
and replacing
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}
if necessary, we may assume that
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}
is an open subscheme of
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}^{c}
.
By Theorem
2.52
and Definition
2.54
,
there exists a morphism
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}^{c}\to\overline{V}^{\mathrm{BB}}
, where
\overline{V}^{\mathrm{BB}}
is as in Setup
4.1
, such that

\mu^{*}\Lambda^{\otimes gk}_{\mathrm{Hodge}}=\mu^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\overline{V}^{\mathrm{BB}}}(d^{\prime})\sim\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}^{c}}(gk\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{c}),

where
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{c}
is the trace of the moduli
\mathbb{Q}
-b-divisor on
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}^{c}
.
Put
L:=h^{0}(\overline{V}^{\mathrm{BB}},\Lambda^{\otimes l}_{\mathrm{Hodge}})-1
.
By Definition
2.54
and embedding
\overline{V}^{\mathrm{BB}}
into
\mathbb{P}^{L}
appropriately, we obtain
L+1
sections

\widetilde{\varphi}_{0},\ldots,\widetilde{\varphi}_{L}\in H^{0}(\widetilde{\mathcal{P}},\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}}(l\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}))

that generate
l\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}
and define a morphism
\widetilde{\mu}\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}\to\mathbb{P}^{L}
such that the linear equivalence
\widetilde{\mu}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{L}}(1)\sim l\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}
holds,
\widetilde{\mu}
factors through the closed embedding
\iota\colon\overline{V}^{\mathrm{BB}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{L}
, and
\widetilde{\mu}
is equivalent to the composition
\iota\circ\mu^{\circ}\circ\rho\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}\to\mathcal{P}\dashrightarrow\overline{V}^{\mathrm{BB}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{L}
as a rational map.
This implies (
2
) of Theorem
4.2
.

The above argument also implies that
l\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}
is a
\mathbb{Z}
-divisor.
By Lemma
2.62
, we have
lK_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{P}}\sim f^{*}D
for some Cartier divisor
D
on
\mathcal{P}
.
Then
lK_{\mathcal{X}_{\eta}}\sim 0
, where
\mathcal{X}_{\eta}
is the generic fiber of
f
.
Note that
X_{\eta}
is also the generic fiber of
f^{c}
.
By construction of the moduli
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor (
[
FM00
]
), we have

l\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}=\rho^{*}D-l\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}
.
Hence,
l\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
is also a
\mathbb{Z}
-divisor.
From this argument, we see that (
1
) of Theorem
4.2
holds.
We finish the proof.
∎

We recall the definition of seminormal schemes and seminormalization.

Definition 4.3

(
[
Sta
, Tag 0EUL]
,
[
GT80
]
)
.

Let
A
be a reduced algebra of finite type over a field.
Then we say that
A
is
seminormal
if for any
x,y\in A
such that
x^{2}=y^{3}
, then there exists a unique element
a\in A
such that
a^{3}=x
and
a^{2}=y
.
As
[
Sta
, Tag 0EUR]
, there exists a unique ring
A^{\prime}
up to isomorphism that is integral over
A
such that
\mathrm{Spec}\,A^{\prime}\to\mathrm{Spec}\,A
is a homeomorphism inducing isomorphisms of residue fields.
We note that an integral homeomorphism inducing isomorphisms of residue fields is a universally homeomorphism.
We say that
A^{\prime}
is the
seminormalization
of
A
.
Note that
A^{\prime}
is contained in the normalization of
A
and hence finite over
A
.

By
[
GT80
, Theorems 1.6 and 4.1]
, we can define the
seminormalization
morphism
\pi\colon\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{sn}}\to\mathscr{X}
for an arbitrary Artin stack
\mathscr{X}
of finite type over a field as the unique morphism such that
\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{sn}}
is reduced and for any étale morphism
\mathrm{Spec}\,A\to(\mathscr{X})_{\mathrm{red}}
from a reduced algebra of finite type over the field,
\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{sn}}\times_{(\mathscr{X})_{\mathrm{red}}}\mathrm{Spec}\,A
is isomorphic to the spectrum of the seminormalization of
A
.
We say that
\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{sn}}
as above is the
seminormalization
of
\mathscr{X}
, and
\mathscr{X}
is
seminormal
if
\pi
is an isomorphism.

By applying Theorem
4.2
, we can deduce the following technical core in this section.
We fix
\iota\colon\overline{V}^{\mathrm{BB}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{L}
, which is induced by
|\Lambda_{\mathrm{Hodge}}^{\otimes l}|
.

Theorem 4.4
.

Set
l:=gkl_{0}
as in Theorem
4.2
.
Then, there exists a morphism of separated Deligne–Mumford stacks

\alpha\colon(\mathcal{M}_{d,v,u,V}^{\mathrm{CYFib}})^{\mathrm{sn}}\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{Kst.qmaps}}_{l(2-u),1,\frac{1}{l},u,\iota}.

To show this, we have to show the following.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
S
be a smooth quasi-projective variety, and let
f\colon(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{A})\to\mathcal{P}
be a family that belongs to
\mathcal{M}_{d,v,u,r,V}^{\mathrm{CYFib}}(S)
such that
\mathcal{A}
is represented by a line bundle.
Take an arbitrary Ambro model
\rho\colon\tilde{\mathcal{P}}\to\mathcal{P}
with respect to
f
, and let
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
be the moduli
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor and the discriminant
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor, respectively.
Let
E
be the
\rho
-exceptional divisor
\mathbb{Q}
-linearly equivalent to
K_{\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}}-\rho^{*}K_{\mathcal{P}}
.
Then,

E+\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}=\rho^{*}(\mathcal{B}+\mathcal{M})

holds and
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}+E
is effective.

Proof.

We first note that
E
is effective since
\mathcal{P}
is smooth by the assumption that
S
is smooth.
Let
\mathcal{B}:=\rho_{*}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
and
\mathcal{M}:=\rho_{*}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}
.
Then, we have

K_{\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}=\rho^{*}(K_{\mathcal{P}}+\mathcal{B}+\mathcal{M}).

By this formula, we also have that

E+\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}=\rho^{*}(\mathcal{B}+\mathcal{M}).

Since
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}
is
\rho
-nef, we see that
\rho^{*}\mathcal{M}-\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}
is effective and
\rho
-exceptional by
[
KM98
, Lemma 3.39]
.
By the definition of the generalized log pair,
\mathcal{B}
is also effective.
Thus,
E+\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}=\rho^{*}(\mathcal{B}+\mathcal{M})-\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}
is effective.
∎

Theorem 4.6
.

Let
S
be a quasi-projective normal variety and let
f\colon(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{A})\to\mathcal{P}
be an object of
\mathcal{M}_{d,v,u,r,V}^{\mathrm{CYFib}}(S)
.
Suppose that
\mathcal{A}
is a line bundle.
Then, there exists the following object
q\colon\mathcal{P}\to[\mathrm{Cone}(\overline{V}^{\mathrm{BB}})\times S/\mathbb{G}_{m,S}]
of
\mathcal{M}_{l(2-u),1,\frac{1}{l},u,\iota}^{\mathrm{Kst.qmaps}}(S)
uniquely up to isomorphism satisfying the following.

(1)

Let
\mathscr{L}
